Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった 2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から 35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券 35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった 2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは 5年に 1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば 5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは 2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を 60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第 2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が 67％を占めていた（資料 1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、「安全運用が基本」とした（注 1）。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注 3）。 GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を 1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は 0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料 2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである 4資産を 25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券 34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ 8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注 11）。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に 3％台に達し、約 30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券 60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、「国内債券の利回りが 3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注 12）。  
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注 13）。 2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

総選挙で揺れるスペイン政治 
政権交代となれば欧州政治・経済にも影響

○ スペインでは総選挙を経て左派から右派へ政権が交代すると予想される。同国は寛容な移民政策
により高成長が続いてきたが住宅不足・価格高騰等の問題が生じ国民の不満が高まっている 
○ 選挙の最大の焦点も住宅や移民であることから新政権は住宅問題を解決すべく移民政策を厳格化
する可能性が高く、今後成長率は減速することが予想される 
○ また、右派政権が成立すればEU内の中道左派の影響力が低下し、移民政策や通商政策、対中政策
等の右傾化・保護主義化が進むことが想定される

１．スペインで前倒し総選挙を実施 
スペインでは11月29日、上下両院の選挙が実施される。2018年から首相を務めるサンチェス氏は、
もともと2027年までの議会任期を全うする方針を掲げていた。こうした中で状況を一変させたのが9月
に発生した大規模抗議活動だ。背景となったのは、住宅不足や家賃・住宅価格の高騰に対する不満だ。
9月下旬には、首都マドリードで長年暮らしてきた高齢女性が家賃の引き上げを巡る裁判で敗訴し、立
ち退きを余儀なくされた。女性が担架で運び出される映像は世間に衝撃を与え、住宅のアフォーダビ
リティ（取得・賃借のしやすさ）低下に対する不満が全国的な抗議活動へと広がった。 
政府は借家人保護の強化などを盛り込んだ緊急住宅問題対策を議会に提出したが、野党の反対によ

り否決された。その3日後の10月5日、サンチェス首相は「さらなる政策を実現するためには、国民の
支持を新たにし、拡大する必要がある」と述べ、議会の早期解散を表明した。 
早期解散の背景には、少数与党政権の議会運営の行き詰まりがあったとみられる。2023年に社会労
働党（PSOE）とSumarによる少数与党政権が成立して以降、政権はカタルーニャやバスクを中心とす
る地域政党の協力に頼ってきた。しかし、独立派政党の一つであるJuntsは2025年10月に政権への支持
を撤回した。このところ、予算の成立がままならない状況が続くなど政治が停滞する中で、同党の反
対により住宅問題対策が否決されたことは、政権が政策の実現に必要な議会の支持を確保しにくくな
っていることを改めて突き付けることとなった。 
また、野党への批判を梃子に議席を積み増す狙いも、このタイミングで解散に踏み切った背景にあ
ったとみられる。サンチェス首相は解散表明の演説で、「国家全体の利益を考える姿勢を欠いた野党
は、我が国の選挙制度の正当性を疑問視し、政策を妨害し、国にとって有益な施策に反対票を投じ続
けてきた」と述べた。さらには、野党を「スペインの民主主義が経験した中で最悪の野党」と厳しく
批判している。住宅問題への関心が高まるなか、「住宅問題の解決を目指す与党」と、「それを阻む
野党」という対立構造を明確に打ち出すことで、与党の支持回復につなげたいとの思惑がうかがえる。 
近年のスペインは左派連立政権のもとで高成長を遂げてきたが（図表1）、選挙を経て政策の方向性
が大きく変われば、同国経済への影響は避けられない。加えて、現サンチェス政権はEUの中で数少な
い中道左派政権であることを踏まえれば、選挙結果はEU全体の政策にも影響を及ぼすことが予想され

る。 
本稿では、選挙の見通しや主要政党の公約を整理したうえで、選挙結果がスペインおよびEU全体の
政治・経済に与える影響を考察する。 
２．右派への政権交代の公算大。住宅問題を受けて移民政策は転換へ 
選挙後は、中道左派・PSOEを中心とする左派連立政権から、中道右派・国民党（PP）が主導する右
派連立政権に交代し、PP党首のアルベルト・ヌニェス・フェイホー氏が首相に就任すると予想される。 
サンチェス首相率いるPSOEは議会第二党の座は維持するものの1、連立相手のSumarを母体とする左
派の選挙連合Frente Amplioの議席は大幅な減少が予想される（図表2）。一方で、現最大野党のPPは議
会第一党の座を維持する見通しだ。PPは単独過半数議席には届かないとみられ、議席の大幅な増加が
予想される極右・Voxとの連立政権を組成し政権奪取を試みることが見込まれる。 
PPが主導する右派連立政権が誕生する場合、どのような政策運営が行われるのだろうか。図表3には
主な公約をまとめている。まず、経済について、PPとVoxはVAT（付加価値税）や所得税、法人税の引
き下げ、行政手続き簡素化等を通じて家計や企業をサポートする姿勢を打ち出している。また、原発
も含めた技術中立的電源構成や電力関連税の引き下げにより、電気料金の抑制を図る構えだ。このほ
か、鉄道網の保守・更新をはじめとするインフラ投資を行うことで、今年に入って相次いでいる鉄道
事故により強まっている安全管理への批判に応えるとともに、交通・物流網の改善を通じて経済成長
にもつなげる方針だ。 
政権交代が生じる場合、大きな政策転換が予想されるのが移民政策だ。前述の通り、スペインは近
年高成長を謳歌しており、この背景にはサンチェス政権による寛容な移民政策があった。サンチェス
首相は、「移民は単なる人道主義の問題ではなく、経済の繁栄と福祉国家の持続可能性にとっても必
要」と発言するなど、移民受け入れを成長戦略と位置付けており、求職ビザの有効期間延長、家族呼
び寄せ要件の緩和、不法移民の大規模な合法化等を行ってきた。流入する移民の約6割が中南米のスペ
イン語圏出身者であり、言語や文化面での障壁が比較的小さいことも労働市場への参加を後押しした。

### Page 4

4

このため、スペイン出身の労働力人口が減少する中でも労働力人口全体は増加している（図表4）。労
働力人口が大幅に増加したことでインフレ下でも実質雇用者報酬はユーロ圏を上回る高い伸びが続き
（図表5）、消費を中心に経済が押し上げられたほか、労働投入の増加により潜在成長率も近年大幅に
向上している（図表6）。Banco de España（2026）は2022～2025年の経済成長のうち約5割が移民の流
入増加に起因すると分析している。 
もっとも、急速な移民流入は、社会インフラのひっ迫という問題も引き起こしている。中でも国民
の関心を集めているのが、議会解散のきっかけにもなった住宅問題だ。同国では、移民の流入による
世帯の増加に住宅供給が追い付かず、足元では約88万戸の住宅が不足していると計算される2（図表7）。
直近2025年の新規住宅供給は約8万戸であり、問題の深刻さがうかがえる。需給のひっ迫が住宅価格
（図表8）や家賃を押し上げ、住宅購入・賃借のハードル上昇や生活苦の要因になっている。実 際、ス
ペイン国立統計局の調査によれば、2025年時点で26～34歳の44.3%が親と同居しており、その47.3%が
住宅購入・賃借する経済的余裕のなさを理由に挙げている。自国が抱える課題を問う世論調査でも、
住宅や移民を挙げる割合が他の欧州主要国と比較して大幅に高い（ 図 表 9）。こうした世論を踏まえて、
PPやVoxはポイント制ビザの導入や不法移民の合法化禁止、受け入れ能力に合わせた移民受け入れ枠
の設置等により移民の流入数を抑え、住宅需要の抑制を目指している。同時に、規制緩和や建設増加
により住宅供給を増やすことで住宅を巡る有権者の不満を解消する構えだ。 
外交分野では、PPはEUや米国、中南米諸国との関係を重視する一方で、中国に対しては貿易不均衡
の是正などを念頭に厳しい姿勢で臨む方針だ。他方のVoxは、党首アバスカル氏とトランプ氏との関
係の近さから対米関係は重視するとみられる。一方で、Voxは国家主権を重視しEU懐疑主義的立場を
とることから、EUの政策が自国の産業や農業に影響を及ぼすことに強く反発しており、欧州グリーン
ディールに基づく規制の撤廃やEU・メルコスール間の貿易協定への反対を掲げている。 
防衛については、PP、Voxのいずれも投資増加を掲げている。ただし、PPはNATOにおける欧州の役
割強化に貢献する方針を示す一方で、Voxは自国の国境管理や治安維持など、自国の主権と領土の保
全を前面に打ち出している。

３．政権交代はスペインのみならず欧州全体の政治・経済にも影響を及ぼす可能性 
政権交代が実現すれば、スペインのみならず欧州全体の政治・経済に影響が及ぶことが予想される。 
近年のスペインの好景気の背景にあった寛容な移民政策は、最大の焦点となっている住宅問題への
対応を目的に厳格化されるだろう。図表6では、資本投入やTFP（全要素生産性）等の条件を一定にし
たうえで、移民流入がサンチェス政権下と同様のペースで続く場合（シナリオ①）、そのペースが半
減する場合（シナリオ②）、流入がゼロになる場合（シナリオ③）の三つのシナリオ別に2026～2030
年の平均的な潜在成長率を試算している。シナリオ①では現在と同程度の潜在成長率が維持される一
方、シナリオ②では①に比べて0.5％ポイント程度、シナリオ③では同1％ポイント弱低下する結果と
なった。PP、Voxのいずれも正規ルートで流入する労働力としての移民受け入れを否定しているわけ
ではなく、近年増加している高スキル人材の流入3は今後も続くとみられることには留意が必要だ。た
だし、住宅供給の増加には時間を要することを踏まえれば、移民流入数の抑制に政策の重点が置かれ
ることも想定される。スペインは近年の高成長により欧州経済を下支えしていたことから、今後は欧
州全体の成長率も減速する恐れがある。 
また、新政権では改善が続いていた財政に負荷がかかる懸念がある。スペインには、コロナ禍から
の経済復興を目的に創設されたEU復興基金（Next Generation EU）から1,000億ユーロ超（GDP比6.7％）
の巨額の資金が割り当てられている。これが自国の財政負担を抑えながら投資を拡大することを可能
にし、高成長と財政健全化の両立に寄与してきた。こうした状況を踏まえて主要格付け会社はスペイ
ン国債の格付けを相次いで引き上げたほか、同国の10年債利回りは政治・経済の停滞が続くフランス
を下回るなど、市場もスペインの経済・財政状況を好感している（図表10）。しかしながら、復興基
金の受け取りは今年末で終了する。こうした中でPPやVoxは各種減税やインフラ投資の実施を掲げて
いる。PPはEU財政ルールの順守を公約しているが、物価高や住宅問題を背景に歳出拡張圧力が強まる
中、財政規律と有権者の要求への対応を両立することは容易ではない。復興基金を用いた投資が細る
ことで成長率の減速が見込まれることもあり、今後財政が悪化に転じる恐れがある。 
PPとVoxが連立を組めば、少数与党であったサンチェス政権とは異なり、議会の過半数を握る公算

### Page 6

6

が大きい。もっとも、それだけで政治の安定を楽観視することはできない。両党には対EU政策や防衛、
移民政策等で調整を要する論点が多く、政策運営において両党の足並みが乱れる恐れがある4。カタル
ーニャやバスクの独立派・地域主義政党との間で、独立や自治権の在り方を巡る対立が強まるリスク
にも注意が必要だ。サンチェス政権は、少数与党であるが故に独立派政党の協力を必要とし、その見
返りとして恩赦などの譲歩を実施した。こうした姿勢は、独立派との関係改善や独立運動の沈静化に
寄与したと考えられる。一方、PPとVoxはともに国家の統一を重視し、自治州における中央政府の役
割強化や独立を目指す住民投票への厳格な対応を掲げており、独立派・地域主義政党との関係が悪化
する懸念がある。また、PPが憲法の枠内で自治州の自治を認める一方、Voxは国家の統一や領土の一
体性を脅かす政党の非合法化まで主張するなど、その姿勢には隔たりがある。独立問題が再び過熱す
る場合、独立派との対立のみならず、その対応を巡る連立内部の意見の相違も表面化し、政権基盤が
揺らぐ恐れがある。 
サンチェス氏は、NATOの防衛費目標への対応や、米国・イスラエルによるイラン攻撃への協力を
巡ってトランプ米大統領と激しく対立してきた。選挙後は、トランプ氏と関係が近いアバスカル氏率
いるVoxが政権入りすることが予想されるほか、PP、Voxのいずれも防衛費増額に前向きな姿勢を示し
ていることなどから、対米関係は改善する可能性がある。もっとも、防衛費に関しては、規模や使途
に不透明な点も残る。PP、Voxのいずれも防衛費の金額規模については明言していないほか、Voxに関
しては、移民対策や治安維持に防衛費を充当する方針を示しており、ロシアの脅威を念頭に置いたEU
やNATOの防衛力強化の方向性とは必ずしも一致しない。また、そもそもスペインはロシアからの距
離が遠く、地政学的リスクが相対的に低いことから、国民の間で防衛・安全保障政策を重視する意識
が低い（図表11）。NATOの目標達成に必要な予算を防衛費に振り向けられるかは、依然として不透明
図表10 10年債利回り 図表11 EU加盟国民の防衛・安全保障の優先度と

### Page 7

7

だ。 
EU政治への影響も小さくないだろう。このところ、EU域内では左派政権の数が減少している（図表
12）。こうした中で、主要国で唯一左派主導の政権を維持してきたスペインで右派への政権交代が実
現すれば、欧州理事会や欧州議会におけるパワーバランスが変化する要因となり得る。サンチェス氏
はこれまで、加盟国首脳が集まりEUの政策の大枠を定める欧州理事会において、中道左派の立場を反
映させる役割を担ってきた。同氏が退陣すれば、EUにおける中道左派の影響力が弱まり、移民政策や
通商政策、対中政策等が一層右傾化・保護主義化する可能性がある。影響は、欧州議会の会派間協力
に及ぶことも想定される。EUでは、欧州理事会の決定を踏まえて、EUの執行機関である欧州委員会が
法案を提出し、EU市民が直接選ぶ議員で構成される欧州議会と、加盟国の閣僚から成るEU理事会が共
同で審議する。サンチェス氏率いるPSOEは欧州議会の第二会派である中道左派の社会民主進歩同盟
（S&D）で最大の国別議員団を構成していることから、同党は欧州理事会における首脳間合意とS&D
をつなぐ政治的接点となってきた。PSOEが下野すれば、S&Dに属するスペイン議員団は首脳間の合意
に歩調を合わせて会派内の意見を取りまとめる動機が薄れることが予想される。その結果、フォン・
デア・ライエン欧州委員会を支える中道右派・中道左派・リベラル会派の協調が揺らぎ、重要法案や
次期中期予算を巡る合意形成が難しくなるリスクがある。また、EU懐疑主義政党であるVoxが政権入
りし、安全保障や移民、脱炭素等の分野で国家主権や国内産業の保護を重視する姿勢が強まれば、EU
内の合意形成が一段と難しくなることが想定される。 
以上のように、スペインでは近年高成長が続いてきたが、その裏で生じた住宅問題等に対する有権
者の不満が政治の不安定化を招き、結果として従来の成長モデルの矛盾と限界が浮き彫りとなった。
新政権は、こうした問題を解決し国民の不満を和らげる政策運営が求められる一方で、移民受け入れ
の厳格化など、有権者の要求に対応するための措置が成長の勢いを弱める可能性がある。また、PPと
Voxの政策路線や独立派・地域主義政党への対応の違いを踏まえれば、議会で過半数を握ることがそ
のまま政権運営の安定につながるとは限らない。
政治・経済の両面で新政権は難しい政策運営を迫
られそうだ。 
右派への政権交代が実現すれば、EU内の政治
的な重心が一段と右に移り、移民政策や財政運営
などを巡る加盟国間の議論にも影響が及ぶ可能
性がある。 
今回の選挙は、スペイン国内の政治情勢にとど
まらず、欧州全体の政策の方向性を考えるうえで
も重要な意味を持つだろう。

1 PSOE は度重なるスキャンダルや北アフリカのスペイン領セウタへの大規模な不法移民流入、少数与党政権であるが故の不安
定な政治運営、今年に入って二度発生している多数の死傷者を伴う鉄道事故等を背景に支持を落としていた。しかしながら、選
挙戦終盤では、右派政権の成立阻止を目指す左派有権者の支持が PSOE に集まっていること等を受けて、同党の支持率はやや持
ち直している。なお、スペインは 1970 年代までフランコによる独裁政権が続いていたこと等を背景に、欧州の中でも極右への
拒否感が強いと言われている 
2 住宅需給のひっ迫には、移民流入による住宅需要の増加に加え、観光客向けの短期賃貸住宅が増え、居住用住宅の供給が減少
したことも影響しているとみられる 
3 川畑・諏訪（2025） 
4 Vo x は、2013 年に当時のラホイ政権（PP）の穏健路線に反発した PP の保守強硬派が立ち上げた政党であるため、両党の間に
は政策スタンスに相違のある分野が少なくない。このため、PP が政権運営上の妥協を模索しても Vo x が強硬姿勢を崩さず、連
立の維持が難しくなる可能性がある。実際、州政府レベルでは移民政策を巡る対立を背景に Vo x が PP との連立を離脱した事例
もある

Feedback: Read-across from
Three-Circle's comments for
Yageo
Positive pricing signals for Yageo: Chaozhou Three-Circle (300408.SZ/6951.HK,
not covered) spoke at our Asia Pacific Summit. It said that its 3Q26 MLCC ASPs rose
20–30% q/q on catch-up increases in mid/low-capacitance products. We see this as

supportive of firmer standard MLCC pricing at Yageo.
Tightness extends into 1H27: Three-Circle said its utilization rate was at 100% and
it expects high-capacitance shortages through 1H27. Management said Murata/
SEMCO’s capacity shifts were happening and this would continue to tighten
standard MLCC supply, supporting our view on Yageo (rated Overweight) and its
utilization and pricing.
Technology limits competitive overlap: Three-Circle said its  AI mix was single-digit,

with small, high-capacitance MLCCs still a capability gap. This suggests limited
immediate competition for the more demanding products within Yageo’s high-
capacitance portfolio.
Three-Circle said its >40% CY26 capacity growth outlook implies >70B MLCC
units/month by year-end, versus ~50B at the start of the year. This compares to
our estimate on Yageo at ~80B/month, although we don't know the exact
specifications used by either company, so that could limit direct capacity

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

ab 23 November 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation
Building streams of growth; Initiate at Buy
Initiate on TW CCL                      - TUC: Attractive Buy on business transition
We initiate TUC with a Buy rating and PT of NT$1,150. The company is one of the
leading suppliers for high-speed low-loss CCL materials with increasing presence more
notably in networking switches, servers, power supply units, and satellites, etc. We

believe these new growth areas will drive 65% revenue CAGR through 2026-2028E;
and anticipate product mix shift toward advanced M7+ materials will drive margin
expansion. We see attractive risk-reward with the stock trading at 6x/4x '27/'28E
consensus PE, vs. historical range of 7-17x, backed by   accelerated earnings expansion.
Enhanced profitability as product mix shifts toward M7/M8+
TUC has relatively less high-end CCL exposure compared to EMC (M7+ was 36% of

2025 sales, vs. 50-55% for EMC) but has a stronger position in high-end networking
switch market with potentially growing exposure in AI ASIC projects. We assume  M7+
CCL product sales contribution to reach 65% by 2030E driven by 1) its expanding
footprint in networking, which is upgrading toward M7/M8+ from 800G/1.6T+; 2)
potential traction in ASIC platforms; 3) power supply related sales contribution  to grow
from 5-10% in H225 to 15%+ in 2027E; and 4) potential opportunities in LEO satellites.

We see differentiated growth engines to drive product mix improvement and margin
expansion. We expect EPS to grow at 89% CAGR through 2026-2028E, outpacing
topline growth of 65% for the same period.
Capacity additions to capture  growth with continued pricing adjustments
Targeting opportunities in the material upgrade cycle fuelled by stronger computing/
networking demand and hence also higher power requirement, TUC is investing for
future growth with capacity expansion planned for Thailand and China. Potential new

CCL entrants currently do not have access to equipment and existing suppliers' capacity
expansion is also constrained by extended equipment lead time (now 2 years+) so overall
industry supply shall remain relatively stable through 2028E. Mid to low-end CCL  pricing
uplifts are expected to increase by at least double digits into H226 with upward trend
sustaining into H127 before new e-glass capacity scales up. M7+ high-end CCL have
started to raise pricing in Q426 to reflect raw material increases, and CCL makers expect

potential further pricing uplifts into 2027E as high-end copper foil and low-DK glass
cloth supply might tighten.
Valuation: Initiate at Buy with PT$1,150
In light of market concerns on AI slowdown, we noted that our estimates are below
consensus factoring in slower capacity ramp (4.6mn/5.2mn in '28/'30E, 10-30% lower
than announced plans) and much less pricing uplifts. Our NT$1,150 PT is based on 8x
'28E PE, which we think is fair at the low-end of historical trading range of 7-17x, and

deem conservative with 36% '26-'30E earnings CAGR, implying only 0.2x PEG.  Despite
more conservatives estimates, we still see good upside on current valuation.

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Is favourable pricing environment and current CCL industry upcycle sustainable?
We continue to see AI CCL content value increases as computing and networking/interconnect
demands surges. Extended equipment lead time and qualification process along with increasing
manufacturing challenges are also suggesting more constrained upstream inputs. We expect

persisting CCL industry shortage to drive better pricing power and believe high-end CCL have
entered a spec-driven structural upgrade cycle, not a temporary commodity uptick.
Q: Could high-end CCL drive the next phase of growth for TUC?
While TUC has relatively less high-end CCL revenue exposure than EMC, we still see multiple drivers
lifting its M7+ product exposure that should lead to better growth visibility and margin profile,
including 1) its expanding footprint in networking, which is upgrading toward M7/M8+ from

800G/1.6T+; 2) potential traction in ASIC platforms; 3)  potential opportunities in LEO satellites. We
expect product mix transition to drive accelerated earnings growth in the next few years.
Among CCL supply chain, we believe TUC is looking to benefit from the structural spec upgrades
cycle, with growing presence in AI server,  not aggressively head-to-head in the hot zones, but actually
seeking growth in a number of diversified areas including networking/switch, power supply, and LEO

satellites, etc. We still see multiple drivers lifting its M7+ product exposure that should lead to better
growth visibility and margin profile.
Top 6 CSP capex spending has revised up another leg, strengthening visibility into AI-related CCL
demand. Hyperscaler capex grew 25% QoQ/ 101% YoY in Q326 to US$252.7bn, with UBS 2026/27E
forecasts revised up 2%/18% to imply +96%/+74% YoY growth to US$897bn/US$1.6tn.
Meanwhile, the pricing backdrop remains supportive,   with Taiwanese CCL suppliers raising prices by

10-30% QoQ in Q126 and Q226, followed by further double-digit increases in August and
September. EMC’s latest commentary reinforces this trend,  noting 5-20% price increases across its
portfolio in Q426F and further upside possible should the supply-demand mismatch persist.
Following its sharp re-rating, TUC has corrected 65% from its recent October peak, versus a 28%
decline in the TAIEX, amid broader tech-sector weakness and profit-taking. The shares trade at 6x/4x

2027E/2028E consensus P/E, with the 6x 2027E multiple at one standard deviation below their
historical 12-month forward average of 12x, offering an attractive entry point. We believe the market
underappreciates TUC’s product-mix and profitability improvement, driven by next-generation CSP
penetration, a richer M8/M9 mix and expanding networking, optical and power exposure, supported
by capacity expansion through 2028. We value TUC at 8x 2028E P/E, supported by 36% 2026–30E

earnings CAGR, implying a 0.2x PEG ratio.

Taiwan Union Technology (TUC) was founded in 1974, and is a leading global supplier of copper-clad
laminate (CCL), prepreg and mass-lamination services for printed circuit boards. The company

specializes in high-speed, low-loss materials, with products used across telecom infrastructure, high-
speed networking, servers and data centers, automotive electronics and LEO satellite applications. In
2025, ELL/SLL (M7/M8) accounted for 36% of CCL revenue, VLL/LL (M4/M6) 32%, HDI/N-LL 24%
and other products 8%. TUC’s manufacturing footprint spans Taiwan, Mainland China and Thailand.

### Page 3

Taiwan Union Technology Corporation UBS Research
Business overview
Founded in 1974 as Taiwan Union Glass Industrial Co., Ltd., TUC initially manufactured
optical glass before pivoting into CCL and prepreg in 1997 and adding mass-lamination
services in 2001.  The company subsequently built its expertise around high-reliability,
low-loss materials, introducing low-loss high-frequency communication laminates in
2008-09 and second-generation very-low-loss products in 2011-15, which established

its presence in 5G base stations and high-end networking equipment.
As network speeds migrated from 100G/400G toward 800G and AI infrastructure, TUC
progressively upgraded its product portfolio from VLL/LL (M4/M6) to higher-end ELL/SLL
(M7/M8) materials for switches, AI servers and CSP platforms, while also extending into
emerging applications such as power, LEO satellites and optical. According to Prismark,
TUC held 16.4% of the global halogen-free high-speed laminate market in 2025,

ranking No. 3 globally. ELL/SLL and VLL/LL products together accounted for 68% of
2025 CCL revenue versus 24% for HDI/N-LL, reflecting its increasing exposure to high-
speed infrastructure applications.

Networking remains a key structural growth driver with 800G/1.6T share gains
TUC was an early supplier to hyperscale networking applications, with one CSP
customer adopting its materials for 100G data-center switches in 2015, followed by two

other major CSPs in 2016. This established networking franchise is becoming
increasingly valuable as switch speeds advance. Having been underrepresented in the
400G generation, TUC is now gaining share in 800G, supported by its relationships with
leading customers in networking/ethernet/switch/router as well as growing penetration
into white-box switch programs. The transition from 400G to 800G and ultimately 1.6T
should continue to drive both share gains and content growth. While 400G primarily

uses M7-class materials, 800G increasingly requires higher-value M8 solutions and 1.6T
uses M8+ solutions alongside greater PCB complexity and rising optical content.
Management noted that recent demand has exceeded the available capacity of several
Korean and Japanese competitors, creating opportunities for TUC to capture
incremental business from customers planning 2027-28 deployments. The company is
also expanding beyond 400G programs into 800G and early 1.6T platforms. Given

networking's superior margins and customers' greater willingness to pay for
performance and secured capacity, we believe TUC is willing to allocate capacity to
800G/1.6T networking products over AI server products.
AI server penetration expands from ODMs to CSP ASICs
TUC initially built its AI server exposure through ODM-led platforms with a focus on
HGX-related boards,  where increasingly open supplier sourcing has supported share
gains. Its opportunity is also expanding as AI server designs migrate from predominantly

M7 toward M8 qualification, raising the value of TUC’s high-speed CCL offering.

### Page 4

Importantly, management views ODM business as particularly attractive given better
pricing and greater influence over supplier selection, and continues to target HGX
opportunities through these customers.
TUC further broadened its exposure into CSP ASIC platforms in 2025 through a major
ASIC customer's project, with penetration continuing on that next-gen project taking
place in 2026. While this represents an important expansion beyond its traditional ODM

base, management noted this ASIC project pricing is less favorable than ODM business,
suggesting the strategic value lies more in expanding TUC’s presence in large-scale ASIC
platforms than in margin uplift. We expect AI server growth to increasingly come from a
combination of deeper ODM penetration and broader CSP ASIC participation.
LEO satellite: OOC capacity positions TUC for further Starlink penetration
LEO satellite build-out is emerging as a key structural growth driver for TUC, supported

by growing exposure to a leading US LEO customer and strategic Thailand capacity. The
customer has accelerated constellation deployment to 11.1k satellites in orbit by Aug-
26, up from c.9.4k when TUC began ramping its LEO business, while end-subscribers
doubled YoY to 12mn, supporting a multi-year demand runway driven by ongoing
constellation expansion and replacement cycles. TUC's current participation remains in
ground-side user terminals, where it emerged as a second source after EMC as the

primary CCL supplier, benefiting from a technology migration from M6-grade TU-883A
to higher-value M7 materials that should drive higher ASPs and gross margins.
Importantly, TUC's Thailand facility has been designated to support the program's out-
of-China (OOC) manufacturing requirements and is well positioned to supply
downstream PCB makers, creating additional share-gain opportunities as capacity
ramps. Beyond user terminals, TUC represents an attractive satellite-side option, where

incumbent CCL suppliers remain Isola and EMC; the company is undergoing
qualification for M8 materials, a step up from the M7 platform used in the prior V3-
generation satellite design. Successful qualification would expand TUC's content
opportunity into higher-value satellite applications and represents upside not yet
embedded in current expectations. We therefore view TUC's LEO growth thesis as a
combination of expanding user-terminal volumes, material upgrades from M6 to M8,

greater utilization of its Thailand OOC capacity, and potential entry into satellite
applications through M8 qualification, positioning the company to capture a growing
share of the rapidly scaling LEO ecosystem.
Strong Positioning in High-Growth Power Applications
Beyond networking and AI servers, power applications have become an increasingly
important growth driver for TUC, accounting for roughly 15% of revenue in Q326, up
from 8% in Q325. The segment, which mainly consists of AI power supply units (PSUs),

but also EV charging and consumer fast-charging applications, has been growing at
nearly 50% YoY, matching the growth rate of the company's M7/M8 business.  UBS
estimates the AI Power TAM will expand from US$18bn in 2026E to US$55bn in 2028E,
supporting demand for power-related CCL as AI infra scales (LINK).
TUC appears particularly well positioned to capture this growth. The company supplies
power-related materials to key customers including one dominant Taiwanese power

supplier that holds ~70% global market share, with Shengyi serving as the primary
second source. Management highlighted that safety and reliability remain critical
considerations, particularly as the industry moves toward higher-voltage architectures,
creating sticky customer relationships and high barriers to entry.
Thicker copper should provide a second growth lever through rising material content
and ASPs. Standard PCBs typically use 1-2oz copper layers, while PCBs with copper

thickness above 3oz (105µm) are generally classified as thick-copper PCBs. Greater
copper thickness reduces electrical resistance and heat generation at a given current,
making it particularly suitable for high-current power applications. Industry
specifications are moving from around 3oz toward 5-6oz as AI power-delivery
requirements increase. TUC has secured thick-copper CCL programs since 2025, and we
expect the combination of higher copper content and more demanding processing

requirements to increase CCL ASPs and material value per board. We view power
applications as an underappreciated growth vector that combines strong market
positioning, structural end-demand and rising content per system.

### Page 5

Key expansion focus on Mainland China and Thailand, with existing capacity in
Taiwan
TUC's manufacturing footprint started in Hsinchu, Taiwan where it started mass
production in 1997 and mass-lamination services from 2001. The company then
expanded into Mainland China, adding its Changshu plant in 2005 and Zhongshan
plant in 2010.
TUC entered 2026 with 2.4mn sheets/month of CCL capacity across Taiwan, Mainland
China and Thailand, following the ramp-up of its first 300k sheets/month in Thailand

end of 2025. A further 300k sheets/month expansion at the Thailand plant began
contributing in July 2026 and is expected to reach full production in Q426, bringing
group capacity to 2.8mn sheets/month by end-2026.
In Mar 2026, TUC announced a new round of capex investment in Changshu and
Thailand, with planned capex of Rmb953mn and Bt6.1bn, respectively. The projects will
add 600k sheets/month at each site, with Changshu expected to commence production

in H127 and Thailand adding 300k sheets/month in each of Q327 and Q427. In Jul
2026, the company announced another expansion cycle for 2028-29, investing
Rmb2.36bn in a new Zhongshan facility for an additional 1.2mn sheets/month and
Bt7.79bn in further Thailand expansion of  additional  750k sheets/month.  We expect
group capacity to reach c.5.35mn sheets/month by end-2028, 5.95mn by end-2029,
and 7.45mn by end-2030  as these projects progressively come online.

Shareholder structure:
Publicly listed on the Taipei Exchange in Dec 2003, TUC was founded by Wen-Bing Hsin
in 1974 and remains controlled by the Hsin family. Current chairman  Chung-Heng Hsin
holds ~3.8% of outstanding shares, while family investment vehicles DingXingFeng Co.
and Chang Yu Hong Co. together hold ~4.7%, bringing total family-related ownership
to ~13%. FINI ownership peaked at ~56% in 2020 amid strong 5G infrastructure

demand, before declining as US restrictions on Huawei disrupted China 5G equipment
production and slowed related CCL demand, followed by 2022-23 electronics inventory
correction. It bottomed at ~21% in 2023 and had recovered to ~39% in 2025/26 on AI/
ASIC re-rating, before easing to ~33% recently (vs Taiex's FINI holding 47%).

Financial analysis
Q326 earnings beat on strong GM expansion
Q326 EPS of NT$12.88 came in above consensus of NT$12.10 on stronger sales and
GM. Sales of NT$17.95bn rose 25.5% QoQ and 122.6% YoY, while GM expanded
7.2ppts QoQ to 36.9%, benefiting from price hikes, product mix improvement and the
earlier-than-expected Thailand capacity ramp, which helped the Thailand operation turn
profitable in Q3. Opex increased 19% QoQ, below the 26% QoQ revenue growth,

supporting operating leverage and OPM expansion to 31.0%; management expects the
opex ratio to decline as revenue scales. By technology, M7/M8 accounted for 38% of
sales, down 1ppt QoQ as price hikes were implemented earlier for mid-to-low end CCL
products, although management noted continued shift towards high-end CCL mix, with
M7 to grow faster than M8 in the near term. M4/M6 fell 3ppts to 28% (+ 13% QoQ),
while HDI & non-low-loss expanded 3ppts to 19% (+49% QoQ), and Others, including

Power, gained 1ppt of mix to 15% (+42% QoQ). Management highlighted particularly
strong growth in Power, a high-ASP, high-margin segment where TUC has strong
competitiveness and sees as one of its key development focuses.
Q426 to see continued pricing momentum.
We forecast Q426 sales of NT$21.24bn, up 18% QoQ, supported by another 100k
sheets of Thailand Phase I capacity following the 200k-sheet ramp in Q326, alongside
tight demand across multiple applications.  We expect GM to remain strong at 37.1% as

high-end product price hikes start to take effect in Q426E, reflecting raw-material cost
increases that TUC absorbed in prior quarters. Management expects sub-M7 product
pricing to remain firm into H127F given continued E-glass cloth tightness, while high-
end copper foil and LDK supply could tighten into 2027 and support further price
increases. We forecast Q426E EPS of NT$16.15, up 25% QoQ.

Pricing and capacity ramp drive earnings growth through 2028E
We forecast TUC’s revenue to grow 80%/51% YoY in 2027/2028E, supported by
substantial capacity expansion and broad-based demand across AI servers, networking,
optical modules, satellite and Power applications. Near-term growth should  benefit from

continued price increases, with hikes in lower-end CCL extending into 2027 and higher-
end products beginning to see price increases. Capacity additions in Changshu,
Zhongshan and Thailand support volume growth, with group capacity expected to
increase 31%/39% YoY in 2027/2028E. TUC is also building qualifications for next-
generation CSP platforms, with current efforts focused on M8/M9 opportunities,
though we expect growth to be stepwise as customer programs move through

### Page 7

qualification and ramp-up. We believe networking and optical applications should
provide additional growth, with optical modules requiring at least M7 and potentially
M9 materials, while PCIe 6 adoption supports further general server demand. We also
expect Power to become an increasingly important driver, given its strong growth,
attractive ASP/margin profile and TUC’s competitive positioning in the segment.
We expect earnings growth to outpace revenue growth through 2028E as pricing, richer

product mix and operating leverage compound the benefit from higher volumes. We
forecast GM to increase from 33.5% in 2026E to 38.7%/40.7% in 2027/2028E,
reflecting a greater contribution from M8/M9 and other high-value products, continued
pricing benefits and improving scale. At the operating level, we expect opex to grow
slower than revenue as the business scales, providing a second layer of margin
expansion, with OPM rising from 27.4% in 2026E to 33.1%/35.7% in 2027/2028E. As a

result, we forecast EPS of NT$92.06/NT$149.51 in 2027/2028E, up 121%/62% YoY
versus revenue growth of 80%/51%, respectively, highlighting the accelerating earnings
benefit from both GM expansion and operating leverage.

### Page 8

Sensitivity analysis
Quantifying earnings downside  risks if AI spending slows down from 2028
The US Department of Commerce measures announced on 16 November seek to
prevent China AI-chip controls from being circumvented through third-country data
centers. The rules extend controls beyond the physical shipment of advanced chips to
who ultimately accesses the compute, while introducing licensing requirements for
advanced-chip exports, re-exports and transfers to Singapore, Malaysia, Indonesia and

Thailand, as well as Brazil, which BIS identifies as presenting higher-than-normal
diversion risk. Chips destined for US-headquartered companies or US-parented
subsidiaries are exempt from this country-level requirement, subject to reporting
conditions. The rules do not altogether prohibit ASEAN AI investment, but raise licensing
and compliance hurdles for parts of the data center ecosystem (LINK). This raises
concerns on first-order impacts of likely to be delayed GPU deployment and slower data

center build-outs. While we believe AI data center build-out remains supported by  cloud
computing power growth, enterprise AI and inference, we note it is constrained by data-
sovereignty and latency requirements, as well as power and land constraints elsewhere.
We see market continues to expect strong 2027 but now more cautious for 2028. We
view some market expectations of a down year might be too conservative as UBS team
has been noting that operating cash flow should support sustainable capex for 2028.

That being said, we still see some sensitivity around potential acts might cap
hyperscalers on securing permitted sites and/or receiving power/water permits.
Below we examine the potential downside risks into 2028 earnings if AI spending
materially slows down vs our current base case scenario. Our key assumptions and
findings are as follows:
 If capacity expansion plan falls short of expectations...: The company has
announced plans to expand CCL monthly capacity from 2.41mn sheets in 2025 to

5.35mn/7.45mn sheets by 2028F/2030F. As we take a more conservative stance to
factor in the AI demand uncertainties, our current model assumes its CCL monthly
capacity of 4.59mn sheets in 2028E, below the company's announced plan of
5.35mn sheets;  and note that if capacity additions were only
50%/30%/20%/10% executed compared to our base case scenario, this will
imply potential 2028E earnings downside of 26%/36%/40%/45% to our current
estimates.

 ...and pricing environment becomes less favourable: We currently expect to
see AI CCL content value increases driven by computing and networking/
interconnect demands; and extended equipment lead time and qualification
process along with increasing manufacturing challenges are also suggesting more
constrained upstream inputs. That being said, if pricing power starts to weaken on
AI spending slowdown, assuming pricing falls short of our current estimates by

5%/10%/15%/20%, on top of lowered capacity, this will imply potential earnings
downside of 32%/45%/53%/60% to our current 2028E estimates.

TUC Share price TUC P/E (x) +1 Std Dev =17x
TUC Avg P/E  12x -1 Std Dev =7x
TUC P/E (x)TUC share price (NT$)
Source: Refinitiv Eikon
TUC’s 12-month forward P/E historically traded below 15x prior to 2023, before re-

rating with the onset of the AI cycle. The re-rating accelerated from 2025 through 2026,
driven by 1) expectations for CCL price increases amid tightening supply, supporting a
stronger margin outlook; 2) growing confidence in TUC's M8/M8+ positioning and
exposure to hyperscaler ASIC programmes; and 3) capacity expansion, including in
Thailand to support higher-end demand and further product mix improvement.  These
factors lifted its forward P/E to a historical peak of 39x in June 2026. Since the AI cycle

began, TUC has traded within a roughly 10–40x forward P/E range, averaging around
17x.
Following its sharp re-rating, TUC’s share price has corrected 65% from its recent peak
since October, versus TAIEX down 28% amid broader tech-sector weakness and profit-
taking following a strong run. TUC now trades at 7x/4x our 2027E/2028E EPS, with its
12-month forward P/E at one standard deviation below its historical average. Our
earnings forecasts are below Street estimates, reflecting our view that TUC’s greater

exposure to mainstream CCL products could leave it more exposed to pricing pressure
and constrain margin expansion. Nevertheless, we expect a gradual improvement in its
product mix: while M7/M8 represented only 36% of 2025 sales, we expect TUC’s high-
end exposure to increase as networking migrates from 800G toward 1.6T,  and higher-
value power and LEO applications contribute more meaningfully. This mix shift, together
with operating leverage, underpins our forecast for gross margin to expand from 33.5%

in 2026E to 40.7% in 2028E and EPS growth to materially outpace revenue growth.
Although we take a more cautious view than the Street on the extent of this
improvement, we believe the current valuation remains attractive even on our
conservative earnings forecasts.
Our NT$1,150 price target is based on 8x 2028E P/E, toward the lower end of TUC’s
historical 7–17x average forward P/E trading range. Our conservative multiple reflects
broader tech-sector selling, rising concerns over slower AI spending and uncertainty

around PTFE adoption, alongside TUC’s relatively lower high-end CCL exposure. Our
target multiple compares with 12x/10x for EMC/ITEQ. We believe the discount to EMC
appropriately reflects TUC’s product positioning, while its improving earnings profile
supports a premium to the broader CCL and PCB groups, which trade at 8x and 7x
2028E P/E on average, respectively.
TUC  is trading at NT$623.00 (as of 20
Nov 2026)

power applications, and capacity expansion. We assume GM of 40.7%, OPM of 35.7%
and EPS of NT$149.5. We apply 8x 2028E P/E to derive our NT$1,150 base-case
valuation.
DOWNSIDE (NT$375): We assume 2028E sales growth of 21.7% YoY, reflecting slower
AI/CSP demand, delays in M8/M9 qualification and ramp-up, and earlier normalization in
CCL pricing as new E-glass supply comes online. We assume GM of 35.7% and EPS of NT

$75.1, reflecting weaker high-end mix and operating leverage. We apply 5x 2028E P/E to
derive our NT$375 downside valuation.

### Page 13

Taiwan Union Technology Corporation UBS Research
Company Description
Company Description
Taiwan Union Technology (TUC) was founded in 1974 and is a
leading global supplier of copper-clad laminate (CCL), prepreg
and mass-lamination services for printed circuit boards. The
company specializes in high-speed, low-loss materials, with
products used across telecom infrastructure, high-speed
networking, servers and data centers, automotive electronics and

LEO satellite applications. In 2025, ELL/SLL (M7/M8) accounted
for 36% of CCL revenue, followed by VLL/LL (M4/M6) at 32%,
HDI/N-LL at 24% and other products at 8%. TUC's
manufacturing footprint spans Taiwan, mainland China and
Thailand.
Industry Outlook
The CCL industry outlook remains positive, supported by AI
server investment and upgrades to high-speed networking
infrastructure. Larger, higher-layer-count PCBs and tighter signal-
integrity requirements are increasing demand for high-

performance, ultra-low-loss laminates, supporting both material
content growth and a richer product mix. Tight supply of
specialty glass fabric and other inputs is supporting laminate
price increases, although rising material costs and supply
constraints remain risks to margins and shipment growth. These
trends should favour suppliers with advanced material
capabilities and reliable access to critical inputs.

Company Description
Taiwan Union Technology (TUC) was founded in 1974 and is a leading global supplier of
copper-clad laminate (CCL), prepreg and mass-lamination services for printed circuit boards.
The company specializes in high-speed, low-loss materials, with products used across telecom

infrastructure, high-speed networking, servers and data centers, automotive electronics and
LEO satellite applications. In 2025, ELL/SLL (M7/M8) accounted for 36% of CCL revenue,
followed by VLL/LL (M4/M6) at 32%, HDI/N-LL at 24% and other products at 8%. TUC's
manufacturing footprint spans Taiwan, mainland China and Thailand.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value TUC using a PE-based methodology.
Key risks include: 1) slower-than-expected high-end CCL adoption and AI infrastructure

deployments or delays in server upgrade cycles; 2) delays in new capacity expansion and
production ramp-up; 3) stronger-than-expected competition and market-share losses in high-
end CCL and power applications; and 4) higher glass fiber, copper foil and other raw-material
costs and weaker-than-expected cost pass-through to customers.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

7717.TT 萊德光電-KY 
 太空資料中心不是夢

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
萊德光電股份有限公司成立於 2014 年 8 月，為光纖被動元件廠商。產品包括光纖通
訊與雷射元件、衛星雷射通訊元件、端面檢測儀與設備、智慧照明等。3Q26 萊德光
電-KY 營收組成為衛星雷射通訊被動元件 57%、光纖雷射被動元件 16%及其它 27%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 495 
【前次投資建議】無 
【大盤指數/股價】36033 / 462

➢ 衛星雷射通訊被動元件帶動 3Q26 營收、毛利大幅成長 
萊德光電-KY 3Q26 營收 3.4 億元(YoY+65.4%，QoQ+1.4%)，營收年
增主因為衛星雷射通訊被動元件營收由 3Q25 的 2 億元(營收比重 48%)，
年增 94% 至 3Q26 的 3.4 億元( 營收比重 57%) 。毛利率 68.6% 
(YoY+10.5ppt，QoQ+4.7ppt)，EPS 2.33 元。3Q26 萊德光電-KY 營收
組成為衛星雷射通訊被動元件 57%、光纖雷射被動元件 16%及其它
27%。低軌衛星客戶訂單持續挹注下，預計 4Q26 營收季增 16.6%至 3.9
億元，預估 2026 年營收 13.2 億元(YoY+53.4%)，毛利率 64.1%，EPS 
10.06 元。 
➢ 2027 年 Starship 載運 V3 引領衛星 LCT 需求成長 
研究員推測 2027 年 SpaceX 發射火箭將由 Falcon 9 改為 Starship 為
主，發射次數約 100 次，載運 V3 數量約 6,000 顆，並預期 Blue Origin、
Amazon LEO 將藉由 Starship、ULA 載運 100 顆、300 顆衛星至太空
軌道，載運總數估達 6,400 顆(YoY+51.1%)。在衛星迭代上，SpaceX V3
新增 Direct-to-Call 功能，上行頻寬約 160 至 200Gbps，下行頻寬達
1Tbps，所需光放大器級數上升，帶動衛星雷射通訊被動元件用量由 3 個
提升至 6 個、9 個。目前公司新舊客戶訂單及研發需求正持續增加，假
設光纖雷射被動元件、智慧照明等營收年增 20%，端面檢測儀與製造設
備出貨 10 台。其中，衛星雷射通訊被動元件營收年增約 50%，營收比
重達 60％，預估 2027 年營收 18 億元(YoY+36.1%)，毛利率 64.3%，
EPS 15.35 元。 
➢ 首次評等給予區間操作，目標價 495 元 
萊德光電-KY 於 2021 年切入衛星雷射通訊領域後，目前在太空雷射元
件領域競爭對手甚少。現階段低軌衛星產業處於發展初期，2026 年至
2030 年衛星發射數量隨著火箭發射數量增加、運載能力增強而以雙位數
CAGR 成長，將逐年帶動萊德光電-KY 衛星雷射通訊被動元件等營收走
高。參考近期低軌衛星產業 Forward PE 評價落在 15X~40X 區間。以
2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 23X，位於區間中緣，故首次評等給予區間
操作，目標價 495 元(2028F PE 25X)。

7717 萊德光電-KY 加權指數

個股報告

公司介紹 
萊德光電股份有限公司成立於 2014 年 8 月，為光纖被動元件廠商，主營運子公司為
設在美國西雅圖的 Lightel Technologies, Inc.。產品包括光纖通訊元件、光纖雷射元
件、衛星雷射通訊元件、光纖端面檢測儀與光纖元件製造設備、智慧照明等。3Q26 萊
德光電-KY 營收組成為衛星雷射通訊被動元件 57%、光纖雷射被動元件 16%及其它
27%。 
圖 1：萊德光電-KY 營收組成

資料來源：國票投顧 
衛星雷射通訊被動元件帶動 3Q26 營收、毛利大幅成長 
萊德光電-KY 3Q26 營收 3.4 億元(YoY+65.4%，QoQ+1.4%)，營收年增主因為衛星
雷射通訊被動元件營收由 3Q25 的 2 億元(營收比重 48%)，年增 94%至 3Q26 的 3.4
億元(營收比重 57%)。毛利率 68.6%(YoY+10.5ppt，QoQ+4.7ppt)，年增 10.5ppt
中，約 4ppt 來自於美國海關退稅款，其餘 6.5ppt 則為高毛利之衛星雷射通訊產品營
收比重提升所帶動。EPS 2.33 元。3Q26 萊德光電-KY 營收組成為衛星雷射通訊被動
元件 57%、光纖雷射被動元件 16%及其它 27%。儘管原本預計在 4Q26 投產之泰國
廠，遞延至 2Q27 開始小量出貨，但在低軌衛星客戶訂單持續挹注下，預計 4Q26 營
收季增 16.6%至 3.9 億元，預估 2026 年營收 13.2 億元(YoY+53.4%)，毛利率 64.1%，
EPS 10.06 元。 
2027 年 Starship 載運 V3 引領衛星 LCT 需求成長 
觀察 2025 年、2026 年(截至 11/20)SpaceX LEO 發射數量各為 3,233 顆、2,300 顆，
火箭發射次數為 170 次、127 次，多以 Falcon 9 運載為主。研究員推測 2027 年 
SpaceX 發射火箭將由 Falcon 9 改為 Starship 為主，發射次數約 100 次，載運 V3 數
量約 6,000 顆，並預期 Blue Origin、Amazon LEO 將藉由 Starship、ULA 載運 100
顆、300 顆衛星至太空軌道，載運總數估達 6,400 顆(YoY+51.1%)。在衛星迭代上，
SpaceX V3 新增 Direct-to-Call 功能，上行頻寬約 160 至 200Gbps，下行頻寬達
1Tbps，所需光放大器級數上升，帶動衛星雷射通訊被動元件用量由 3 個提升至 6 個、
9 個。目前公司新舊客戶訂單及研發需求正持續增加，假設光纖雷射被動元件、智慧
照明等營收年增 20%，端面檢測儀與製造設備出貨 10 台。其中，衛星雷射通訊被動
元件營收年增約 50%，營收比重達 60％，預估 2027 年營收 18 億元(YoY+36.1%)，
毛利率 64.3%，EPS 15.35 元。

累計發射數量 2024年 2025年 2026F 2027F 2028F
SpaceX 7,503 10,737 13,036 19,036 26,836
Amazon LEO 2 182 398 698 1,198
One Web 656 656 656 656 656
總計 8,161 11,575 14,090 20,390 28,690
2028 年建立太空資料中心，AIDC LEO 為成長動能 
展望 2028 年，SpaceX 可能於外太空建立約 5GW AI 運算能力，並在軌道部署約 1
萬個 Nvidia GPU 機架。目前 SpaceX 等低軌衛星廠商申請 AIDC 衛星數量已超過 100
萬顆。基於每顆 AIDC 衛星要與前後、左右衛星相連，需配置 6 顆 LCT，較現行衛星
增加 3 顆 LCT，而單顆 LCT 需要 6~９個光纖被動元件(包含合束器、隔離器、波長分
波多工器、濾波器、分光器與端帽等)，因此單顆 AIDC 衛星光纖被動元件用量由 18~27
個增至 36~54 個，帶動萊德光電-KY 產值增為原本的 2 倍至 3 倍。除了 AIDC 衛星
之外，地表的光基地台將以高功率雷射幫助低軌衛星傳遞訊號及能量，解決低軌衛星
太陽能板重量大、供給效率低的問題，並建置太空與地表連接的 6G 寬頻全光網路，
此亦是雷射被動元件營收成長動能。假設 2028 年 SpaceX Starship 發射次數達 130
次，V3 載運總數達 7,800 顆(YoY+21.9%)，光纖雷射被動元件、智慧照明等營收年增
15%至 20%，端面檢測儀與製造設備出貨維持 10 台，在泰國廠營收貢獻提升下，預
估 2028 年營收 23 億元(YoY+27.5%)，毛利率 64.3%，EPS 19.81 元。 
太空雷射對 LEO 發展不可或缺，首次評等給予區間操作，目標價 495 元 
萊德光電-KY 於 2021 年切入衛星雷射通訊領域後，目前在太空雷射元件領域競爭對
手甚少。現階段低軌衛星產業處於發展初期，2026 年至 2030 年衛星發射數量隨著火
箭發射數量增加、運載能力增強而以雙位數 CAGR 成長，將逐年帶動萊德光電-KY 衛
星雷射通訊被動元件等營收走高。參考近期低軌衛星產業 Forward PE 評價落在
15X~40X 區間。以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 23X，位於區間中緣，故首次評等給
予區間操作，目標價 495 元(2028F PE 25X)。

2026.11.17 
吳昆潮 C00269@ibfs.com.tw 
7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元，低於預期 
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利率 27.97%，
稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 2.83 元，低於投研部預期，
主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台
興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個
百分點；2)高價原料成本衝擊：白銀在 2026 上半年曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史
天價，公司 8~9 月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料，對 8~9 月的單月毛利率造成短期壓抑。

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

Document 328: European Economics Analyst_ UK Outlook 2027_ Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

### Page 1

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Growth accelerated n
despite rising energy prices and tighter financial conditions, and the labour 
market showed signs of stabilisation. The new government navigated a difficult 
fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key budget that maintained the consolidation 
path. 
The coming quarters are likely to be more challenging. First, we expect growth to n
slow as the impact of the energy shock on consumer spending increases over the 
winter. Second, inflation will rise further as electricity and gas bills increase and 
the indirect effects of the energy shock build. We expect this to push the BoE to 
hike a second time, probably in February. Third, the government faces a difficult 
Spending Review; existing spending plans look too tight, but the Chancellor has 
limited fiscal headroom. 
Nonetheless, we see compelling reasons to be constructive on the economic n
outlook for 2027. First, some of the recent growth improvement looks structural, 
and the productivity trend is strengthening. We therefore expect activity to 
reaccelerate later in the year as some of the cyclical headwinds fade, and our 
Q4/Q4 growth forecast is above consensus at 1.4%. Second, we do not expect 
strong second-round effects from the energy shock into domestic inflation, so 
the BoE is likely to hike significantly less than priced. Third, we expect further 
fiscal consolidation, and a more favourable OBR forecast could ease the 
trade-offs facing the Chancellor. 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic n
prospects to improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its 
strengths in professional services, and surveys indicate that companies expect a 
material boost to productivity growth in the coming years. The PM has also 
recently raised the possibility of closer EU ties, which could bring notable 
economic gains over time.

### Page 2

UK Outlook 2027: Cyclical Headwinds, Strengthening Fundamentals

The UK economy proved surprisingly resilient in 2026. Despite the energy shock keeping 
inflation elevated and causing the BoE to pivot from cutting to hiking, growth 
accelerated this year. The labour market showed signs of improvement, with 
unemployment falling and employment growth indicators ticking up. The new 
government navigated a challenging fiscal backdrop to deliver a low-key Budget that 
maintained the consolidation path.

Cyclical Headwinds 
The coming quarters are likely to prove more challenging in several respects. First, we 
expect growth to slow in the near term and run slightly below trend in Q4 and the first 
half of 2027. The impact of the energy shock on household finances is likely to build over 
the winter, with weak real disposable income growth resulting in a slowdown in 
consumer spending.

Exhibit 1: Growth Proved Surprisingly Resilient in 2026

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 3

Tighter financial conditions should also weigh on growth as mortgages are refixed at 
higher rates. Fiscal policy will likely turn more contractionary compared with this year 
given that current plans imply a larger consolidation in the primary balance in 2027.

While the labour market data improved over the last few months, we expect slower 
near-term growth to result in a modest further weakening. That is consistent with 
continued softness in most indicators of employment intentions. The pace of loosening 
should be gradual as the peak impact of last year’s labour cost increases on employment 
growth has probably passed. But we see the unemployment rate rising back to 5% by the 
middle of next year.

Exhibit 2: Consumer Spending Growth Likely to Slow

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 3: Growth Headwinds from Rising Mortgage Rates and Tighter Fiscal Policy

Note that student loan figures for 2026-27 are affected by the accounting impact of a freeze in repayment thresholds, so the figures excluding student loans may 
give a better guide to the impacts on demand.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, OBR
15 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 4

Second, inflation is likely to increase further in the coming months. Consumer energy 
prices will rise notably in January on the back of higher electricity and gas bills. We also 
expect food and core goods inflation to accelerate as the indirect effects of the energy 
shock become more apparent. We also see renewed upward pressure in some regulated 
services prices that are indexed to headline inflation, and higher fuel costs will likely raise 
transport services prices. Overall, we expect inflation to peak at 4.3% in February and 
average 4.1% in 2027H1.

The peak in headline inflation will likely exceed the threshold at which the BoE has 
typically become more concerned about second-round effects. Taken together with 
hawkish recent commentary, that points to another 25bp BoE hike to 4.25%. This will 
probably come in February, although an earlier December move is also possible.

Exhibit 4: Renewed Labour Market Softness

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England

Exhibit 5: Rising Inflationary Pressures Point to a Second BoE Hike

The forecasts shown on the left are conditional on GS forecasts for energy prices.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   4
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 5

Third, the government faces a difficult Spending Review. Existing plans imply a notable 
slowdown in departmental spending growth from next year onwards, which looks 
challenging to deliver given pressures on the health and defence budgets. History 
suggests that these plans will probably be notably upgraded. New policy priorities could 
require further outlays, with PM Burnham’s agenda likely to become clearer after the 
publication of his 10-Year Plan. But financing higher spending may be challenging given 
limited fiscal headroom, with revenues constrained by Labour’s manifesto not to raise 
taxes on working people. Welfare reforms could bring some savings over time but look 
likely to be accompanied by upfront investment in employment support.

Structural Improvement 
Despite these cyclical challenges, we see several reasons to be more constructive on the 
UK’s economic prospects for 2027. First, some of the tailwinds that supported growth 
this year look structural. Strength in business investment has partly reflected rising 
information and communications capex, which looks likely to be sustained by ongoing 
data centre investment. This investment should support continued growth in technology 
services output, where demand has increased on the back of AI adoption. Further 
growth in utilities capital spending linked to renewables should also continue to support 
demand.

Exhibit 6: A Challenging Spending Review

Source: OBR
15 November 2026   5
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 6

The productivity trend also appears to be improving, as growth in output per worker has 
increased significantly recently. Some of this may reflect the impact of last year’s 
employer National Insurance hike on hiring, but more recent data also show gains in 
sectors such as professional services that were less affected by labour cost increases. We 
also see more fundamental reasons to expect a pickup. While Brexit has probably 
weighed significantly on growth over the past decade, the worst of the headwind has 
likely passed. Survey data from the Decision Maker Panel indicate that early gains from 
AI adoption could also have modestly boosted the figures.

These factors, together with declines in energy prices, should mean that growth 
reaccelerates through the second half of next year. Overall, we expect a 1.4% Q4/Q4 
increase in GDP, above consensus expectations as well as the BoE and OBR forecasts.

Exhibit 7: Some Growth Drivers Look More Structural

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Yotzov et al. (2026)

Exhibit 8: Trend Productivity Growth is Probably Improving

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Various Academic Studies
15 November 2026   6
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 7

Second, we continue to expect limited second-round effects into domestic price 
pressures. Even after the recent decline in the unemployment rate, broader measures of 
utilisation (such as the vacancy/unemployment ratio) suggest that the labour market 
remains far looser than in 2022/23. That will likely reduce the extent to which higher 
headline inflation passes through into wage pressures. Indeed, early data indicate that 
pay settlements are likely to remain stable next year, and the BoE’s Decision Maker Panel 
survey shows little change in wage expectations since the start of the Middle East 
conflict. Private sector regular pay growth will probably accelerate from low levels but is 
likely to remain well below the levels seen after the previous energy shock.

With only a modest increase in pay growth, we expect overall labour cost inflation to be 
well below the levels seen in recent years. This is especially true in consumer-facing 
sectors, which saw sharper increases in wages over recent years and the largest impact 
from the employer National Insurance increases. More moderate labour cost inflation

Exhibit 9: Our GDP Forecast is Slightly Above Consensus

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg, Bank of England, OBR

Exhibit 10: Second-Round Effects Likely to be Moderate

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bank of England
15 November 2026   7
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 8

should help to keep a lid on underlying services inflation, despite cost pressures from 
higher energy and food prices.

Given moderate second-round effects, we think that the BoE will hike less than priced. 
After tightening policy in February, we expect the MPC to keep Bank Rate at 4.25% for 
the rest of the year, whereas the market implies two further rate increases. The risks 
around our baseline are skewed towards further tightening, but even on a 
probability-weighted basis our forecast is notably below current market pricing.

Third, 2027 is likely to see renewed progress on deficit reduction. The current fiscal 
plans imply a steep reduction in the primary deficit next year. While we think spending 
could overshoot the official projections, tax receipts could also come in somewhat 
higher. Further consolidation should mean that the UK continues to compare favourably 
to other advanced economies in terms of reducing borrowing. While the Spending 
Review does pose a challenge for the government, it is possible that changes in the

Exhibit 11: More Limited Labour Cost Increases Should Keep a Lid on Underlying Services Inflation

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, ONS

Exhibit 12: We Expect the BoE to Hike Less Than Priced

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Bloomberg
15 November 2026   8
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 9

economic forecast could help the Chancellor. The OBR’s wage growth forecast continues 
to look low, and an upgrade would boost the tax revenues projection, increasing fiscal 
space.

A Positive Long-Run Outlook 
We also see scope for sentiment around the UK’s longer-term economic prospects to 
improve. The UK is well-placed to benefit from AI adoption given its specialisation in 
high-value added services. IMF estimates indicate that a relatively high share of the 
workforce is exposed to AI, with a particularly large number of workers in roles that may 
see productivity gains but are less likely to be displaced. Survey data suggest that firms 
expect adoption to result in sizeable productivity increases in the coming years. Even 
though these gains are unlikely to be fully additive to the current productivity trend, the 
numbers still indicate upside risks to our estimates for supply growth.

Exhibit 13: Continued Fiscal Consolidation

On the left, the UK data are shown on a public sector basis.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, OBR, Bank of England
15 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 10

The UK is also well-positioned in several high growth innovative industries. This includes 
a world-leading position in life sciences, such as pharmaceuticals, biotechnology, 
medical technology, and genomics. The UK has a world-class research base, with the 
London/Oxford/Cambridge triangle a leading biotech cluster. 
Another possible source of long-run growth upside would be closer ties with the EU. The 
upcoming (November 20) summit could see some progress, with the two sides expected 
to finalise agrifood and emissions trading deals. But the Prime Minister has also 
indicated that he will use the summit to outline options for how the relationship might 
develop after the next election, potentially including a customs union, rejoining the 
single market, or even a second referendum on membership. Any reset would require 
negotiation, political trade-offs, and time before benefits materialise. But it could bring 
significant economic upside, so signals towards improved trading links could start to lift 
optimism on the UK’s growth prospects.

Exhibit 14: Well-Placed to Benefit from AI Adoption

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, IMF, Bank of England

Exhibit 15: Signals Towards Closer EU Ties Could Boost Sentiment on Long-Run Growth

Document 334: First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-26).pdf

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New
Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 November 23 2026 Share
The September quarter saw a larger than expected rise in spending, including firmness in
core categories.
September quarter retail sales
Retail sales (volume of goods sold): +2.2% (Prev: -0.4%)

### Page 2

Westpac f/c: 1.4%, Market: +0.5% 
Core retail sales (volume of goods sold): 1.4% (Prev: 0.8%)

Nominal retail sales: 2.4% (Prev: 1.0%)

Year to September
Volume of goods sold: 3.5%
Nominal sales: 7.0%

New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall.
The September quarter retail sales report showed a sharp recovery in spending. Nominal spending
was up 2.4% over the quarter, and the amount of goods that New Zealanders are actually taking home
was up a solid 2.2%.
Today’s result was above our top of market forecast for a 1.4% quarterly rise, and well above the
median market forecast for +0.5%.
While the strong September quarter result is welcome news, we are taking it with a grain of salt. Much
of the rise in spending was related to fuel spending, which was boosted by the fall in fuel prices over
the September quarter, and fuel prices have step higher since then.
However, even excluding fuel, we have seen firmness in spending appetites. Core spending (excl. fuel
and motor vehicles) was up 1.4% over the September quarter and is 4.7% higher than this time last
year. 
Looking at the breakdown of spending, we did see a rise in grocery purchases. Meanwhile, spending in
discretionary categories was mixed – there has been lower spending on items like furnishings and
hardware, while spending on hospitality, electronics and recreational items is up. Overall, were left with
a picture of gradually improving spending appetites.

Outlook
Today’s report reinforces our expectations that the economy is moving into stronger phase. We expect
that spending levels will continue to firm through the holiday season and 2027. However, that recovery
is still likely to be gradual due to the combination of ongoing high inflation, a soft labour market and
rising interest rates. We also expect the recovery will be uneven, with those regions with strong links to
the agricultural and tourism sectors outperforming metro regions, like Auckland and Wellington.

Implications for GDP growth
We’re forecasting GDP growth of 0.6% in the September quarter. Today’s result was above
expectations. We’ll take a closer look at how our forecast for GDP growth is shaping up over the next
couple of weeks as additional data on September quarter activity is released.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

Document 344: GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026年 11月 9日

1/3 
GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし 
～2026年 9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. 2026年 7～9月の収益率はマイナス 1.65％ 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）は 2026年 11月6日、同年 7～9月の運用実績を公表
した。期間収益率はマイナス 1.65％だった。7～9月は国内外の長期金利が上昇し、債券価格が下
落した。加えて円高も進み、外国資産の円換算額を押し下げた。資産別の収益率は国内債券がマ
イナス 1.80％、外国債券がマイナス 6.52％、国内株式がプラス 3.71％、外国株式がマイナス
1.86％となった。9月末の運用資産額は314兆3,876億円と 6月末から約 3.4兆円減少した。 
GPIFを巡っては、片山さつき財務・金融相が 7月に「日本の金融資産にさらなる投資をしてい
ただくという方向で後押しをする方策を追求したい」と発言し、ポートフォリオに変化があるか
注目が集まっていた。長期金利が上昇していたこともあり、特に国内債への関心が高かった。 
結論から言うと、国内債比率は基本ポートフォリオの 25％に極めて近く、運用姿勢の変化を読
み取ることは難しい。国内債の比率を引き上げるのではないかという観測は一旦後退するだろ
う。

2. 国内債券比率は 25％近辺を維持  
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは国内外の株式と債券をそれぞれ 25％とし、国内債券には±6ポイ
ントの乖離許容幅が設定されている。GPIFの公表資料によると、年金積立金全体に占める国内債
比率は 2026年9月末時点で 26.49％だった。これには年金特別会計で管理する積立金、約 5.4兆
円が含まれている。 
この積立金を国内債券と年金積立金全体から除き、GPIFの運用資産ベースで計算すると、9月末
の国内債券比率は25.23％となる。6月末の 24.98％から 0.25ポイント上昇したが、基本ポートフ
ォリオの25％をわずかに上回る水準だ（資料1）。 過去の実績と比較しても、今回の比率は突出し
ていない。2020～2025年度の 9月末平均は 25.05％で、2026年9月末はこれを0.18ポイント上回
る程度だ。

### Page 2

2/3 
資料 1 GPIFの運用資産構成比率（推計、2026年 6月末・9月末）

（注）年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いたGPIF運用資産ベース。 
（出所）GPIF「2026年度第 1四半期運用状況（速報）」、「同第 2四半期運用状況（速報）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成。

3. 国内債券の買越額は推計 1.4兆円  
次に、7～9月の国内債券への買越額を推計する。6月末と 9月末の国内債券残高の変化から、
期間中の運用損益による増減を除いて推計した。 
年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いた国内債券残高は、6月末の 79兆3,840億円から 9月末
の79兆3,206億円へ、634億円減少した。一方、7～9月の国内債券の運用損益はマイナス 1兆
4,452億円だった。この差額から、国内債券への買越額は約 1.4兆円と推計される（注 1）。4～6
月の約 5.7兆円と比較すると、国内債券への資金配分額は減少した（注2）。 
同じ方法で他の資産への資金配分を試算すると、外国債券は約 3.3兆円の買い越しとなった。
一方、国内株式は約2.0兆円、外国株式は約 0.8兆円の売り越しとなった。

4. 国内債券の運用姿勢に変化はみられず  
2026年7～9月の運用実績をみる限り、GPIFが国内債券の乖離許容幅を積極的に活用し、基本ポ
ートフォリオの25％を上回る水準へと比率を引き上げようとする動きは確認できなかった。 
国内債券は買い越しているとみられるものの、外国債券には国内債券を上回る資金が配分され
ている。4資産の比率はいずれも25％近い。基本ポートフォリオに近づけて運用する従来の姿勢
が維持されていると考えられる。 
以 上

Document 392: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

Document 402: Spain’s election promises collide with a shrinking fiscal margin

Source PDF: all report pdf/Spain’s election promises collide with a shrinking fiscal margin.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 10
